    Measured combinations¶

    Which component works under which method, measured: every cell of this page is a recorded run of grasppanda compose and grasppanda probe, and nothing is inferred from a contract or from another cell. The contract tables of Swapping components say what the config check accepts; this page says what happened when the accepted combinations were built, trained and asked to predict. A refused cell is the verdict of the config check on that config: nothing was built. The combination explorer answers a selection of method and options from these records; the slot pages say what each option is.

    A cell is one run, and a run is not repeatable. The two recorded commands repeat the experiment, not its numbers: the config hash and the parameter count repeat exactly and the losses of the first iteration to the digits given; later losses, the share of real and of supervised seeds, the grasps per frame and, for a combination near the edge, the split between runs and runs-unstable are those of the recorded run and differ in the next one. A record is bound to the commit of its run: it is replaced when its config changes, not when the code of a component does.

    No AP appears on this page, and none follows from it. A probe trains 20 iterations on 8 frames. It shows whether a combination works: it builds, its losses are finite, seeds reach its head, it predicts. It says nothing about how good the combination is; that takes a full training run and an evaluation on a benchmark split. Whether a cell that runs also learns like the stage it replaces (250 iterations, term by term): Learns like the reference?.

    What was run¶

    • Grid: 1262 recorded cells (1014 runs, 174 runs-empty, 29 runs-unstable, 41 refused, 4 fails): every shipped method config as it is, every registered scene encoder under every seed-based method, every variant of every other stage with one stage replaced at a time, a sample of the legal sets of stage variants, HGGD as it is, every image-encoder preset under HGGD, every post-processing preset under all six methods, every recipe preset under every method it applies to, every loss preset in one term of every kind it accepts under every seed-based method with such a term, and a few requests that replace several slots at once. The variants of a stage are its presets plus every component whose constructor defaults no preset states. A component that needs an option only a preset supplies (DepthEmbedGroup, DepthPoolGroup, MultiRadiusGroup, MultiScaleGroup) has no cell under its type name: that request is refused for the missing option before any contract is compared, which says nothing about a combination. Its presets have cells.
    • A cell: grasppanda compose <method config> --set <slot>=<choice>, so that an encoder is fitted and adapters are inserted exactly as a user gets them, then grasppanda probe <composed config> --iters 20 --frames 8 --schedule constant: the real trainer with the method's own recipe on the split sample of GraspNet-1Billion (RealSense; scene 0, frames 0, 32, …, 224), then prediction on two of those frames. Both commands of every cell are in the measured file.
    • The learning rate: grid probes train at the recipe's peak rate without its schedule (--schedule constant: the optimizer's rate, or max_lr of a one-cycle schedule, at every step, scaled by the ratio of the batch sizes when the probe's batches are smaller than the recipe's, so FineGrasp's 0.008 for 32 frames trains at 0.002 on 8; optimizer, weight decay, clipping and hooks as the recipe has them, except that a batch-norm momentum the recipe decays per epoch is held at its first-epoch value, as the rate is held at its peak: a probe's epochs are a few iterations long). Run over the few epochs of a probe, the schedules would decay GSNet to 0.63 and EconomicGrasp to 2.4 % of its rate and keep FineGrasp in its warm-up, and the trends of different methods would not compare. A cell whose request sets the schedule (a schedule preset, a schedule-free optimizer, a recipe preset) runs that schedule. grasppanda probe --schedule recipe runs a recipe as shipped. Of the 1182 probes on this page, 1103 held the peak rate (schedule: constant in the record); 79 ran the recipe's schedule (schedule: recipe).
    • One exception to "as a user gets them": configs that read the width histogram of Scale-Balanced-Grasp were probed with the reference's own file (scale_statistic.source: legacy_import), because the native histogram is a statistic of the whole training split and a probe reads eight frames. The override is part of the recorded command.
    • When and where: 3 cells on 2026-10-15 with GraspPanda at commit 0ddca31, PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118, Python 3.12.7, on one NVIDIA RTX A6000 shared with other jobs, 2 probes at a time. 288 cells on 2026-10-15 with GraspPanda at commit 2a3760a, PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118, Python 3.12.7, on one NVIDIA RTX A6000 shared with other jobs, 2 probes at a time. 538 cells on 2026-10-15 with GraspPanda at commit 96facee, PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118, Python 3.12.7, on one NVIDIA RTX A6000 shared with other jobs, 2 probes at a time. 430 cells on 2026-10-15 with GraspPanda at commit 98e5310, PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118, Python 3.12.7, on one NVIDIA RTX A6000 shared with other jobs, 2 probes at a time. 3 cells on 2026-10-16 with GraspPanda at commit 96facee, PyTorch 2.7.1+cu118, Python 3.12.7, on one NVIDIA RTX A6000 shared with other jobs, 2 probes at a time.
    • Two cells with one config are one experiment. A cell that composes to the very config of another cell (the same config hash) shares its probe; one that composes to its method's shipped config is marked own. 39 of the 1262 cells share a probe.
    Group Cells probed runs runs-empty runs-unstable refused fails

    Verdicts¶

    Verdict In a table Meaning
    runs ✓ · ✓ own · ✓ in Adapter On real frames: the detector was built, every requested training iteration ran with finite losses and at least one real seed, every top-level part of the model that holds trainable parameters received a gradient in at least one iteration, the weights stayed finite, and prediction returned one valid grasp group for every probed frame, at least one of them with a grasp.
    runs-empty ∅ · ∅ in Adapter As runs, except that no probed frame has a grasp: prediction returned one valid grasp group for every probed frame and every group is empty. Every stage ran; a model trained for a few iterations often keeps no seed. A run that is also unstable is runs-unstable.
    runs-unstable ≈ (n) On real frames: the detector was built, trained and predicted, but a loss or a weight became non-finite (a non-finite loss ends the training there), or an iteration had no real seed (no scene point passed the proposal, so only the proposal was trained), or no iteration supervised a seed, or a top-level part with trainable parameters received no gradient in any iteration, or fewer iterations ran or fewer frames were predicted than were asked for.
    refused ✗ (n) The config check refused the config (the same check as grasppanda print-config --check --for train). Nothing was built; the report carries the messages. On this page all 41 refusals come from grasppanda compose, which runs that check on the composed config and writes nothing when it fails.
    fails fails (n) An exception was raised after the config check had passed. The report names the phase (build, data, optimizer, loss, backward, step, hook, predict), the component it came from and the message.
    wired — No real frame was read. On a synthetic table-top scene with random labels the detector was built, back-propagated a loss through every stage and predicted. This shows that the stages connect; it says nothing about real data. Not used on this page.

    ✓ in Adapter: the bare swap is refused by the contract between encoder and proposal; compose wrapped the encoder in the one adapter that bridges it, said so, and the adapted config ran. †: compose also fitted the encoder to the method, setting out_channels to the width of the encoder it replaces or in_channels to the point features the method feeds. (n) points to the reason below the table: for a refusal the first sentence of each message of the config check (the whole messages, with the fix they propose, are in the measured file), for a failure the phase, the component and the exception, for an unstable run what was unstable. ‡ marks numbers that depend on the load of a shared machine. § marks what judge 2 flagged in a run that did not stop it from running (Judges). Below the mark of a probe: the grasps it predicted on each probed frame and, for a record of judge 2, the range of the fall of its loss terms and the levels it did not reach (learns ✗ (n)); a probe that predicted no grasp on any frame is greyed. Below a refusal, its kind when the record has one (structural, not-implemented, policy, not-a-combination).

    Judges¶

    A probe is judged by grasppanda.combine.probe. The verdicts above and the fields below are those of judge 2, from 2026-10-15; 1221 of the 1262 records on this page were judged by it (judge: 2 in the measured file). The other 41 were judged by the first judge. It had no runs-empty, so a run that predicted no grasp on any probed frame is runs (0 probes of this page); it recorded none of the fields below; and from 2026-10-13 on it also checked a run for a top-level part that no gradient reached, which only a record with no_gradient shows (0 of them). A record keeps its judge until its cell is probed again.

    Judge 2 also records whether the parts of a model learned, not only whether they ran. None of it changes a verdict, except that a top-level part no gradient reached makes a run runs-unstable (as before) and an empty prediction makes it runs-empty. In a record of judge 2:

    Field Meaning
    judge 2 for a record judged by judge 2, which holds the fields from trend to lr_factor; absent for a record of the first judge, which holds none of them (about.judges)
    trend per loss term and for the total loss, over the iterations in which the term was active (reported, finite and not exactly 0.0): [first, last_quarter, fall], its value at the first of them, the mean of the last ceil(active / 4) of them, and (first - last_quarter) / |first|, positive when the term fell (0.7: by 70 %), to four significant digits and three decimals; three nulls when the term was never active
    active per loss term active in fewer iterations than ran: the number it was active in
    flags per loss term with a flag: the flags of the list below that hold
    no_gradient the units of the model whose gradient never had an L2 norm above 1e-8 in an iteration, over its trainable parameters. A unit is a registered sub-module at depth 2 (backbone.encoder, head.score_net) or a parameter a top-level part holds itself (head.bias); when no unit of a top-level part was reached, the part is listed instead of its units (backbone: a dead part). Gradients are read before every optimizer step, unscaled under mixed precision. In a record of the first judge (only when its probe had the check): the top-level parts without a non-zero gradient
    unchanged the top-level parts whose trainable parameters did not change: the L2 norm of the difference between before the first optimizer step and after the last one is at most 1e-6 times the larger of 1 and the L2 norm before
    checked [units, parts] with trainable parameters, over which no_gradient and unchanged were checked
    score_quantiles per predicted frame, the smallest, the median and the largest score of its predicted grasps, to 4 significant digits; null for a frame without a grasp. Scores are those the detector returns (their meaning is its score_kind)
    learning_rate, lr_factor the learning rate of the first parameter group of the first optimizer at the first and at the last optimizer step, as those steps used it; the last of these divided by the first: the decay the schedule applied within the probe. A schedule that steps per epoch decays with the probe's epochs, which follow from the batch size and the number of frames (GSNet's 0.95 per epoch: 0.95^9 after 20 iterations of 2 per epoch); 1 under the schedule constant; null when the first is 0
    schedule, rate how the probe set the learning rate (about.schedules): constant, the recipe's peak rate at every step, or recipe, the recipe's schedule run over the probe's epochs (always for a cell whose request sets the schedule); rate is the rate every step used under constant, in the first parameter group of the first optimizer. A record without schedule was probed before the probe had the option, with the recipe's schedule
    losses_per_iteration not in the record: the side file measured_combinations/losses_per_iteration.json holds, for every record of judge 2 with a probe of its own, every loss term and the total at every iteration, to four significant digits (null where the term was not reported). It is written with the records and holds what the trends are computed from

    The flags of a loss term; active iterations are those in which the term was reported, finite and not exactly 0:

    • flat: the term was active in at least two iterations, and the mean of its last quarter differs from its first active value by less than 5 % of that value.
    • rising: the term was active in at least two iterations, and the mean of the last half of its active iterations exceeds the mean of the first half by more than 10 % of the first half's mean (with an odd count the middle iteration is in neither half).
    • zero_at_end: the term is exactly 0.0, or not reported, at the last completed iteration.
    • constant: the term was reported in at least two iterations with the very same value in every one of them, zeros included.

    The schedules of a probe (schedule in a record):

    • recipe: the config's own scheduler, run over the probe. The probe sets train.epochs to ceil(iterations / iterations per epoch), so a schedule that steps per epoch is compressed into those few epochs (GSNet's 0.95 per epoch ends at 0.95^9 = 0.63 after 20 iterations of 2 per epoch, EconomicGrasp's cosine at 2.4 % of its start) and one that steps per iteration runs its first steps only (FineGrasp's warm-up of 800 iterations keeps a probe of 20 at no more than 2.5 % of its rate).
    • constant: no scheduler: every parameter group of every optimizer trains at the recipe's peak rate for the whole probe. The peak is the rate the optimizer gives the group (the configured lr, with the multipliers of ParamGroups), except for a schedule that sets a peak of its own: max_lr of OneCycleLR and CyclicLR, the larger factor of LinearLR. Every other schedule of the toolbox starts at that rate or reaches it at the end of its warm-up. When the probe's batches hold fewer frames than the recipe's, the peak is scaled by their ratio (the linear scaling rule: FineGrasp's 0.008 for 32 frames trains at 0.002 on batches of 8). A batch-norm momentum that the recipe decays per epoch (BNMomentumHook: GraspNet-baseline, Scale-Balanced-Grasp, HGGD) is held at its first epoch's value likewise (hooks.<i>.decay=1): decayed over epochs of a few iterations it can reach its floor of 0.001 within the probe (Scale-Balanced-Grasp's at iteration 73), and the running statistics, which prediction normalises with, then stop following the weights; training itself does not read them. The optimizer (its weight decay and its momentum as configured), clipping, accumulation and every other hook are the recipe's.

    Fall per loss term §, a column of the tables of what the probes saw, gives the fall of every term (the term without loss/; active n where it was active in fewer iterations than ran), and Learns, predicts § whether the run reached those levels (Measured classes) and what kept it from them: the flags, the units no gradient reached, the parts that did not change, frames without a grasp or with one score for all; not checked for a record of the first judge. A flag is a statement about one short run, a reason to look, not a verdict. Under schedule: constant every step of that run used the recipe's peak rate, so flags compare across methods; a record without schedule ran the method's own schedule over the probe's epochs, which may have lowered the rate within the probe (lr_factor) by very different amounts from one method to the next.

    Measured classes¶

    One run, seed 0, 20 iterations, 8 frames of one scene; a class counts verdicts, not accuracy. A cell (one option under one method) is decided at four levels, each from its record alone; every level needs the one before it:

    • composes: the contract rules and the config check accept the composed config: grasppanda compose writes it (any verdict but refused). A refusal by policy is undecided (needs consent), and so is a request that is not a combination.
    • runs: the probe ran clean on real frames: the verdict runs or runs-empty. A runs-unstable probe counts as not running only when its repeat (repeat in the record: the same config, seed and schedule) was unstable too; without a repeat the level is undecided (unstable, not repeated); when the repeat ran clean the cell runs, and learns and predicts are read from the repeat. fails does not run.
    • learns: judge 2 only. Every loss term was active (reported, finite, not exactly 0) in at least 50 % of the iterations and fell by at least 5 % (fall = (first - mean of the last quarter) / |first| over its active iterations), and no term is flagged flat, rising or constant; a term that is 0 at the last iteration (zero_at_end) is judged on its active iterations. Every unit with trainable parameters received a gradient (no_gradient is empty) and every part changed (unchanged is empty).
    • predicts: judge 2 only. Every predicted frame has at least one grasp, and on every frame the smallest and the largest score differ (score_quantiles): a model whose scores collapsed to one value ranks nothing.

    A level that cannot be decided from a record says why:

    • not checked: the record was judged by the first judge, which recorded neither the trend of the loss terms nor the scores of the predicted grasps.
    • unstable, not repeated: the probe was runs-unstable and has not been repeated: an unstable run counts as not running only when a second probe of the same config is unstable too.
    • needs consent: refused by a policy that waits for the user's consent (trust_remote_code needs accept_remote_code: true): a rule about this machine, not about the combination.
    • not a combination: the request names a component that lacks an option only a preset supplies: it is refused before any contract is compared, and is no combination.
    • not probed: the grid has the cell, but no record yet.
    • not reached by the method as shipped: the method as shipped, probed the same way, does not reach this level either (for example FineGrasp at the constant rate, whose fusion of crop scales gives every seed one feature in a short run, so every grasp gets one score): under that method the level says nothing about the option at this budget.

    The class of an option at a level counts the methods under which it reached that level. For a stage of the seed-based skeleton decided under all five seed-based methods (GSNet, EconomicGrasp, FineGrasp, GraspNet-baseline, Scale-Balanced-Grasp):

    • universal: it reached the level under all five seed-based methods.
    • most: under three or four of them.
    • some: under two.
    • specific: under exactly one.
    • none: under none of them.

    An option of another slot (post-processing, loss, recipe, image encoder) is universal only when it reached the level under every method it applies to and those include the five seed-based ones. Every other class reads N of M probed: it reached the level under N of the M methods where the level was decided, and the methods where it was not are named after it (+2 not checked). A level that no method decided is named by the reason. A level decided only where the level before it failed is not checked: the class says so instead of counting the failures again.

    A class is a statement about this grid: these presets, the methods' own recipes, this dataset, one stage replaced at a time. A stage that is refused alone may work together with the stage it depends on (a head bundle with its assigner and its labels); those are the sets of stages. The classes of the scene encoders and of the other stages, level by level:

    Class: one run, seed 0, 20 iterations, 8 frames of one scene; a class counts verdicts, not accuracy.

    Encoder adapters¶

    An adapter wraps an encoder and is not one itself, so it has no row above: compose inserts it where the contract between encoder and proposal asks for it (✓ in Adapter). What was measured with each, over all groups of the grid:

    Adapter Cells it was inserted in runs runs-empty runs-unstable refused fails Under

    Class: one run, seed 0, 20 iterations, 8 frames of one scene; a class counts verdicts, not accuracy.

    1. CompactLabelAssigner returns 'compact' matched labels, but SWADHead is supervised with 'dense' ones. data.train.dataset: CompactLabelAssigner reads supervision.extras['compact_labels'], which this training dataset does not serve.
    2. CompactLabelAssigner returns 'compact' matched labels, but OperationHead is supervised with 'dense' ones. ObjectnessViewStage computes its targets from 'dense' matched labels, but CompactLabelAssigner returns 'compact' ones. data.train.dataset: CompactLabelAssigner reads supervision.extras['compact_labels'], which this training dataset does not serve.
    3. NearestLabelAssigner returns 'dense' matched labels, but EconomicHead is supervised with 'compact' ones. data.train.dataset: NearestLabelAssigner reads supervision.objects, which this training dataset does not serve.
    4. ScaleBalancedAssigner returns 'dense' matched labels, but EconomicHead is supervised with 'compact' ones. data.train.dataset: ScaleBalancedAssigner reads supervision.objects, which this training dataset does not serve.

    The training recipe¶

    A recipe preset changes what is around the model: the optimizer, the schedule, the hooks of the training loop, the point augmentation. Each is applied alone to every method as shipped and probed like a stage; an augmentation a method's data contract does not accept is refused there. —: the preset names the scene encoder of the seed-based skeleton, which HGGD does not have. A preset that sets the schedule (the schedule presets, the schedule-free optimizers, the recipes) is probed with that schedule, schedule: recipe in its record, since the schedule is what its cell measures; the others with the schedule of the grid (A cell above). What each preset is and how to use it: the recipe slot.

    What did not run, in the words of the tool:

    • recipe/GSNet/optimizer/schedulefree-sgd (runs-unstable): 1 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 3).
    • recipe/EconomicGrasp/optimizer/schedulefree-sgd (runs-unstable): 1 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 3).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/optimizer/schedulefree-sgd (runs-unstable): 3 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 15, 17, 19). No grasp was predicted on any probed frame.
    • recipe/HGGD/optimizer/soap (fails): at step: RuntimeError: CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered CUDA kernel errors might be asynchronously reported at some other API call, so the stacktrace below might be incorrect.
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/constant (runs-unstable): 4 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 2, 3, 10, 11).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/cosine-restarts (runs-unstable): 2 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 2, 3).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/cosine (runs-unstable): 3 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 2, 3, 12).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/multistep-warmup (runs-unstable): 2 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 3, 4).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/poly (runs-unstable): 2 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 3, 4).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/schedule/warmup-cosine (runs-unstable): 2 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 3, 4).
    • recipe/GSNet/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by GSNet yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/EconomicGrasp/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by EconomicGrasp yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by EconomicGrasp yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/GraspNet-baseline/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by GraspNetBaseline yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/Scale-Balanced-Grasp/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by ScaleBalancedGrasp yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/HGGD/hooks/amp (refused): train.amp: mixed precision is not supported by HGGD yet: its loss is not written for half-precision tensors (the detector does not declare amp_ready = True).
    • recipe/FineGrasp/hooks/freeze-encoder (runs-unstable): 2 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 1, 5).
    • recipe/GraspNet-baseline/hooks/swa (fails): at hook, in the hook SWAHook: EngineError: SWAHook.start_epoch is 7, but the run has 5 epochs: nothing would be averaged.
    • recipe/Scale-Balanced-Grasp/hooks/swa (fails): at hook, in the hook SWAHook: EngineError: SWAHook.start_epoch is 7, but the run has 5 epochs: nothing would be averaged.
    • recipe/FineGrasp/augment/dropout (fails): at data, in the transform DepthNormals: ContractError: DepthNormals must run before the cloud of graspnet1b is sampled: put it first in transforms.
    • recipe/HGGD/augment/jitter (refused): data.train.dataset.transforms: RandomJitter moves the points, but this dataset section also serves image-space data (rgb, depth, view_grasps), which would stay where it was.
    • recipe/HGGD/augment/shift (refused): data.train.dataset.transforms: RandomShift moves the points, but this dataset section also serves image-space data (rgb, depth, view_grasps), which would stay where it was.
    • recipe/FineGrasp/recipe/finetune-encoder (runs-unstable): 1 of 20 iterations had no real seed (iteration 1).

    Sets of stages¶

    The contract rules accept 9104 sets of stage variants, too many to probe each: 95 were probed (86 runs, 9 runs-empty) and 9009 were not. The probed sets were picked so that a stage variant that occurs in a legal set occurs in a probed one, under a dense and under a sub-sampling encoder: 118 of the 118 pairs of variant and kind of encoder are in a probed set. That a set runs says nothing about another set with the same parts. The sets that were not probed are the sets of grasppanda.combine.grid.cells(("stage-set",)) without a record (about.stage_sets_not_probed of the measured file counts them); python -m tools.probe_grid run --only '<id>' probes one.

    A set states all five stages and the encoder: the preset named, under which the set needs no adapter. It was composed onto the method config whose data recipe serves its labels (last but one column), so the optimizer, the schedule and the data pipeline are that method's.

    Other detector families: one component replaced¶

    A detector that is not built on the seed-based skeleton has slots of its own; HGGD has one, the encoder of its image network (Image encoders). Every preset of such a slot was composed into the family's shipped config and probed:

    Post-processing¶

    Every post-processing preset as the evaluation variant post of every method (grasppanda compose <config> --set post=<preset>), applied by the probe to the predictions of its two frames: grasps predicted → grasps left. A post-processor reads grasps and the observation, never a network, so it needs no training of its own: a cell is the method's prediction plus the chain. The grasps are those of a detector trained for the iterations of the probe; the page of the slot has the same steps on the predictions of released weights.

    Losses¶

    Every loss preset in every kind of loss term it accepts, under every seed-based method that has a term of that kind (grasppanda compose <config> --set <stage>.<loss option>=<preset>). Which loss fits which kind of term is a contract (Heads and losses); the unit tests run every loss in every term of its kind on tiny models. A method has several terms of some kinds, so a cell replaces one representative term per kind and method: the last term of that kind in the order of the pipeline (proposal, view, head; within a stage, the order the stage declares its terms in). That is the term nearest the grasp, where the methods differ; GSNet, EconomicGrasp and FineGrasp share their proposal. The representative terms:

    Scale-Balanced-Grasp head.angle_loss — head.tolerance_loss

    Several slots at once¶

    The tables above replace one thing at a time, and the stage sets keep the encoder to two. These cells replace an encoder together with options of other slots (a grouper, a proposal, a view stage, a recipe preset, a post-processing preset), each as one request: grasppanda compose <method config> --set <slot>=<option> --set …. They are a hand-picked sample, at least two per method, of what a visitor composes; a pair or a triple that is not listed here was not probed, and its verdict does not follow from the cells of its parts. The explorer shows what the config check answers for any two options.

    Not on this page¶

    • Accuracy of any kind. See the method pages of the model zoo.
    • Other datasets, cameras and recipes than the ones named above.
    • Combinations of two or more replaced options beyond the stage sets that were probed and the cells of Several slots at once.
    • Inner parts of HGGD: it has no registered slots yet, so it is probed as it is.

    Adding a component¶

    A new encoder or stage appears in the grid by being registered. Give it a preset if its options cannot be guessed (Try a combination), probe its cells and regenerate:

    python -m tools.probe_grid run --only 'encoder/*/MyEncoder' 'stage/*/*/my-preset'
python -m tools.probe_grid export
python -m tools.make_combination_docs

    tests/unit/test_combine_grid.py fails until every cell of a registered component has a record in the measured file.
